Key design: Logical relation for strong normalization. A common approach (Tait's method / Girard's reducibility) adapted to locally nameless with `has_type` using cofinite quantification.

We need:
- Define `reducible` / `SN` for terms. But note: strong normalization is a property of closed terms. The logical relation usually is defined on closed terms.

Approach options:
1. Define `sn : tm -> Prop := strongly_normalizing` (the given inductive). Then define the logical relation by recursion on type:
   - `LR Bool t := strongly_normalizing t /\ (value t -> t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false)`? Hmm, for CBV strong normalization we need more than SN: for the fundamental theorem, the abs case needs to show that an abstraction applied to a value of the argument type is SN. Actually let's think.

Standard Tait-style for CBV:
```
R_Bool = { t | SN t }
R_{T1->T2} = { t | SN t /\ forall v, value v -> R_{T1} v -> R_{T2} (app t v) }
```
Hmm but `SN t` for an abstraction in CBV... An abstraction is a value, and SN of a value means all its reducts are SN; a value has no reducts except through... an abstraction itself doesn't step. So SN(abs) holds trivially. But then application `app (abs T t) v --> open t v` requires showing open t v is SN. That's the fundamental theorem part: if `t` is in R_{T1}->R_{T2} and `v` in R_{T1} with value v then `app t v` is SN. For an abstraction, `app (abs T t) v --> open t v`; so we need `SN (open t v)`. This comes from the typing of body and substitution lemma / fundamental theorem on the opened body.

Alternative approach: Use a "strongly normalizing" closure with a condition that application to any term in R argument yields SN. A cleaner known approach: define `R` on all terms (not just closed):
- `R Bool t := SN t`
- `R (T1 -> T2) t := SN t /\ (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> SN (app t v))`? Actually if `t` is SN and `t --> t'`, then `app t v --> app t' v`? Wait, ST_App1 requires `locally_closed t2` (t2 = v). Hmm. So we need closure of the relation under step: if `R T t` then `SN t`, and if `t --> t'` and `R T t` then `R T t'`? For fundamental theorem we need the relation to be "reducible"/closed under expansion: if `t --> t'` and `R T t'` then `R T t` (head expansion). Hmm.

Alternative approach: Use the "candidate" definition by induction on types with a closure under "expansion" (a.k.a. reducibility candidates):

A set S of terms is a reducibility candidate if:
- S ⊆ SN
- if t ∈ S and t --> t' then t' ∈ S (closure under reduction)
- if t is "neutral" (not a value) and all its one-step reducts are in S then t ∈ S (head expansion).
- values of function type...

Lemma R_app_SN : forall T1 T2 t1 t2,

    SN t -> lc t ->

  In many formalizations (e.g. "A formal proof of strong normalization" for call-by-value), the logical relation for CBV is defined as:
  ```

V Bool v := SN v (and v is true/false?)
V (T1->T2) := { v | forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u) }
T T t := SN t ∧ forall v, t -->* v -> V T v  -- "t is SN and every value it reduces to is in V"
```
This is the standard "reducibility candidates" for CBV: T T t := SN t ∧ (forall v, t -->* v -> V T v). Then:
- SN t is part of it, so the fundamental theorem gives SN.
- Closure under reduction: if T T t and t --> t' then T T t' (since SN and multistep-to-value conditions).
- For arrow: V (T1->T2) v := forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u). Since v is a value (abstraction), the app reduces as expected.
- Fundamental theorem for app: T T2 (app t1 t2) from T (T1->T2) t1 and T T1 t2. Hmm, we need SN (app t1 t2) and all its value reducts in V T2.
  For the value reducts: if app t1 t2 -->* v, then... in CBV, t1 -->* v1 (value), t2 -->* v2 (value), and then app v1 v2 -->* v (v1 = abs). Then V T2 v? To get that, we need app v1 v2 ∈ T T2... Let's see: T (T1->T2) t1 gives all value reducts of t1 are in V (T1->T2), and T T1 t2 gives all value reducts of t2 in V T1. Multistep decomposition lemmas needed. Then V (T1->T2) v1 applied to... hmm, we need T T1 v2 — values in V T1 imply T T1 v2 (since v2 is a value reducing to itself, and SN?). And then T T2 (app v1 v2); then its value reduct v (app v1 v2 -->* v) is in V T2. This works!
  
  This is the classic "Tait-Girard with SN + value interpretation" that handles CBV well. The key lemmas:
  - `V T v -> T T v` for values v (needs SN v).
  - `T T t -> t --> t' -> T T t'` (closure under one step).
  - `T T t → t -->* v → V T v`.
  - `V T v -> value v` maybe.
  
  Actually, this style is often used for weak normalization; for strong normalization, T T t := SN t ∧ ... works because SN is built in and closure under reduction is easy.

Let me examine the crucial direction: the fundamental theorem for the abstraction case. We have a body semantics: for all sigma satisfying Gamma extended with x:T1, R T2 (subst sigma (open body (fvar x))), where R = T (term interpretation). Need to show V (T1->T2) (abs T1 body') where body' = subst sigma body, i.e., for all u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app (abs T1 body') u).

So we need: SN (app (abs) u) and every value reduct of it is in V T2.

For the value reducts: if app (abs) u -->* v, then u -->* v2 (value) (since app (abs) u steps: App2 until u is a value, then beta). More precisely multistep decomposition: either u -->* v2 value and then v = ... open body' v2 or app... since after u reaches value, beta step gives open body' v2, then open body' v2 -->* v. Hmm, open body' v2 is not a value necessarily; it can reduce further. So to show V T2 v, we need that T T2 (open body' v2) (then its value reduct v is in V T2). And T T2 (open body' v2) comes from the body's semantics instantiated at sigma' = sigma[x->v2]! But this requires x fresh in body (composition lemma) and v2 ∈ T T1 with... hmm, but the body semantics gives T T2 (subst sigma' (open body (fvar x))) which equals T T2 (open (subst sigma body) v2) = T T2 (open body' v2).

But wait, the body semantics hypothesis requires v2 ∈ T T1 (the semantic value of x in the extended environment), and we have T T1 u, hence u -->* v2 gives V T1 v2 by the multistep-value lemma, and V T1 v2 should imply T T1 v2. Yes! So this works, and it doesn't need considerations of "R T2 (app (abs) u)" directly being proven by SN induction on u; instead the multistep decomposition and the value interpretation handle it.

    Let me define:
    ```
    Fixpoint V (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
      value v /\
      match T with
      | Ty_Bool => True  (or v = true \/ v = false)
      | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u)
      end
    ```
    But V and T are mutually recursive: T refers to V and V refers to T. Since ty is an inductive and the recursion descends on T, we can define them mutually by a Fixpoint with two outputs? Coq's Fixpoint can define mutually recursive functions over the same inductive using `Fixpoint ... with ...`. But here V and T both take ty, and the recursive calls are on smaller types: T (T1->T2) calls V (T1->T2) (same type!), and V (T1->T2) calls T T1 and T T2, and T T1 calls V T1 (smaller). The call T (T1->T2) -> V (T1->T2) is on the same type, but structurally it's fine if they're defined as one mutual fixpoint: V and T are two functions, and the call from T to V at the same type argument is a call to a different function in the mutual block with the same decreasing argument — Coq's guard checker should accept mutual fixpoints where each call decreases structurally. Yes: in a mutual Fixpoint, calls between the functions must have a structurally smaller argument (unless it's the same? no, must be strictly smaller). Hmm, T (T1->T2) calling V (T1->T2) with the same argument is not structurally smaller, which may violate the guard condition.

    Alternative: make V defined inside T's definition as a match:
    ```
    Fixpoint T (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
      SN t /\ lc t /\ (forall v, multi step t v -> V' T v)  -- where V' is defined in terms of T
    ```
    But V' T v for arrow needs T T1 and T T2 — recursion on smaller types, OK. And T T calls V' T at the same type. If V' is a separate function that T calls, guard fails. But we can inline V as a `match` inside T:
    ```
    Fixpoint T (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
      SN t /\ lc t /\
      (forall v, multi step t v -> value v /\
        match Ty with
        | Ty_Bool => True
        | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u)
        end)
    ```
    Here T's body calls T T1 and T T2 where T1, T2 are subterms of Ty (only in the arrow branch, where Ty = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 and T1/T2 are subterms). This is accepted by the guard checker since the recursive calls are on structurally smaller arguments. The match on Ty with recursive calls in branches is fine.

    But then we lose a named V; we can define V as a derived notion:
    ```
    Definition V (Ty : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
      value v /\ match Ty with
                 | Ty_Bool => True
                 | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u)
                 end.
    ```
    But this V refers to T, which is defined before. And T's definition inlined the same formula rather than calling V. Then we need lemmas connecting them: `T T t <-> SN t /\ lc t /\ forall v, t -->* v -> V T v`. That's provable by reflexivity/simplification, but the unfolding may need care with the match. Hmm, maybe better to define them as a mutual Fixpoint with V calling T on smaller types and T calling V on the same type — let's test whether Coq accepts:

    ```
    Fixpoint V (Ty : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
      value v /\
      match Ty with
      | Ty_Bool => True
      | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u)
      end
    with T (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
      SN t /\ lc t /\ forall v, multi step t v -> V Ty v.
    ```
    The call `T Ty t` -> `V Ty v` at the same Ty: in a mutual fixpoint, the guard condition allows calls to another function in the block with the same argument? I believe the guard condition requires the argument to be a subterm for mutual calls too... Actually, Coq's guard condition for mutual fixpoints: recursive calls must be on structurally smaller arguments, where "structurally smaller" is defined via the inductive subterm relation. Calling T Ty from V Ty doesn't decrease. Hmm, but there's the possibility of defining V inside T via a nested fixpoint or a `let`.

     So define:

     Fixpoint T (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=

             **Decomposition lemma (general application)**: If `app t1 t2 -->* v` and `value v`, then there exist v1 v2 such that `t1 -->* v1`, `value v1`, `t2 -->* v2`, `value v2`, and `app v1 v2 -->* v`.              **Decomposition lemma for abstraction application**: If `app (abs T body) u -->* v` and `value v`, then exists v2, `u -->* v2` ∧ `value v2` ∧ `open body v2 -->* v`. This is a special case of the general one with v1 = abs (t1 is a value, multistep refl).
             
             So we can prove one general decomposition lemma and use it both for T_App and T_app_abs. Let me state it:
             
             `app_multistep_value : forall t1 t2 v, multi step (tm_app t1 t2) v -> value v -> exists v1 v2, multi step t1 v1 /\ value v1 /\ multi step t2 v2 /\ value v2 /\ multi step (tm_app v1 v2) v.`
             
             Hmm, is it true that `multi step (app v1 v2) v` with v possibly... Let's check: after t1 -->* v1 and t2 -->* v2, we have app t1 t2 -->* app v1 v2 (by congruence). And app t1 t2 -->* v by hypothesis. By determinism (CBV is deterministic), v = the result of app v1 v2's evaluation, so app v1 v2 -->* v. We could either prove determinism of step and use it, or prove the decomposition directly by induction on the multistep derivation. Direct induction:
             
             `Lemma app_multistep_value : forall t1 t2 v, app t1 t2 -->* v -> value v -> exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ value v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ value v2 /\ app v1 v2 -->* v.`
             
             Proof by induction on the multistep derivation (multi). 
             - multi_refl: app t1 t2 = v, and v is a value. But app is not a value; contradiction (need lemma `value_app : value (app ...) -> False`).
             - multi_step: app t1 t2 --> w -->* v. Case on the first step:
               * ST_AppAbs: t1 = abs T body, t2 = v2 value, w = open body v2. Then app t1 t2 -->* v means open body v2 -->* v. We need witnesses v1 = t1 (abs, value), v2 = t2 (value), and app v1 v2 = app (abs) v2 -->* v: one step AppAbs to open body v2 (needs lc abs — from ST_AppAbs's hypothesis) then multistep. Good.

Let me reconsider the problem from scratch with a cleaner known technique. I recall the standard proof of strong normalization for simply typed lambda calculus with CBV in Coq (e.g., from the "Coq'Art" or various courses) uses Tait's method with the logical relation:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Bool => SN t
  | T1 -> T2 => SN t /\ forall t', SN t' -> ... 
```

Hmm. In many textbook presentations with *call-by-name* or general beta, the logical relation is:
R_Bool = SN
R_{A->B} = { t | SN t ∧ ∀u. R_A u → R_B (t u) }
and the key lemma "R_A t → SN t" plus "R is closed under expansion". The proof of the application case uses the fact that t ∈ R_{A->B} gives R_B (t u) for u ∈ R_A, and SN is a conjunct, so no circularity (the SN of t u is part of R_B (t u) which is given by the hypothesis). The abstraction case uses: for all u ∈ R_A, R_B ((abs body) u). In call-by-name, `(abs body) u --> body[u]`, and the body semantics gives R_B (body[u]). To show R_B ((abs body) u), one shows R_B is closed under *backward* reduction / "head expansion": if t --> t' and R_B t' then R_B t. This head expansion lemma is proven by induction on the type B: for Bool, SN is closed under head expansion (if all reducts SN then SN); for arrow, need SN and the app property: for u ∈ R_A, (abs body) u --> body[u] u? Hmm, in call-by-name, ((abs body) u) v --> body[u] v, and R_B (body[u]) gives the app property for all v, so R_B (body[u] u?) no wait, R_B (body[u]) means for all v ∈ R_A, R_B (body[u] v)? Hmm, the app property of R_B is over the *term* body[u], and ((abs body) u) v reduces to (body[u]) v, so head expansion at B works.

For CBV, the difference is that `(abs body) u` does not reduce until u is a value. So the head expansion lemma doesn't directly apply for non-value u. BUT: call-by-value has the property that if u ∈ R_A and u is not a value, we can consider its reducts... The standard solution for CBV is exactly what I was doing: use the SN induction on u (since R_A u -> SN u), or define R in a "value-based" way.

Actually, here's a cleaner idea for CBV: define R on values directly (V) and define the term relation as "SN + all value reducts in V", which is the approach I described. This is the standard "reducibility candidates à la Tait for CBV" (also known as "Girard's method adapted to CBV"). The key point is that the term-level relation is defined via an SN induction on... Let me look at the structure we've derived:

```
T Ty t := SN t ∧ lc t ∧
  match Ty with
  | Bool => True
  | Arrow T1 T2 => (forall u, T T1 u -> SN (app t u)) ∧ (forall v, t -->* v -> value v -> V Ty v)
  end
V Ty v := match Ty with
          | Bool => True
          | Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u)
          end
```
and the equivalence T Ty t ↔ SN t ∧ lc t ∧ (forall v, t-->*v -> value v -> V Ty v) plus the extra SN-app conjunct for arrows.

For a value reduct v' of `open body v` — well, the function `app abs u` reduces to `app (open body v) w` eventually... Since v_f is a value and a value reduct of f, and T B f? Do we have T B f = T (B1->B2) (app abs u)? That's exactly what we're trying to prove (circular). Hmm.

OK here's the thing: we need a way to prove SN (app f w) where f = app abs u without assuming T B f. We know SN f and the structure of f's reduction. The value reducts v_f of f are: f -->* v_f means u -->* v2 (value), then open body v2 -->* v_f. Since v_f is a value, T B2? Hmm, we need SN (app v_f w) where v_f : value, w ∈ T B1. If v_f is an abstraction (as a value of type B1->B2), then app v_f w reduces via beta to open body_f w; and we'd need SN of that... but we don't know the body of v_f semantically.

Hmm, this seems to require more: we need "SN (app v_f w) for all value reducts v_f of f" which itself needs a semantic argument about v_f. But v_f is a value of type B1->B2 and we know... hmm, do we know V (B1->B2) v_f? That's what T B f's third component would give. Circular.

OK, let me step back and think about how the standard proof handles this. I think the key is that the logical relation for CBV is usually defined with the application property for *values* only, and the term relation is "all value reducts are in the value relation". Then the fundamental theorem works as follows:

V(T1->T2) v := forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u)  (v a value)
T T t := SN t ∧ forall v, t -->* v -> value v -> V T v

For T_Abs: need V (T1->T2)(abs) = forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app abs u).
Proof of T T2 (app abs u):
  - SN: SN_app_abs (induction on SN u; beta case uses Hbody and SN of open body v for values v).
  - value reducts: decompose app abs u -->* v; get v2 with u -->* v2, value v2, open body v2 -->* v. Then T T1 v2 (closure), Hbody v2 : T T2 (open body v2), whose value-reduct component gives V T2 v. Done.
This is clean! Now what about T_App (the typing rule): need T T2 (app t1 t2) from T (T1->T2) t1 and T T1 t2:
  - SN (app t1 t2): This is the problematic part. Hmm.
  - value reducts: decompose; get v1, v2 with t1 -->* v1, value v1, t2 -->* v2, value v2, app v1 v2 -->* v. T (T1->T2) t1's value-reduct component gives V (T1->T2) v1 = forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v1 u) (note: no value requirement on v1 in V's definition? V should include value v1 implicitly since it's only applied to values). Apply to v2 (T T1 v2 by closure): T T2 (app v1 v2), then its value-reduct component gives V T2 v. Done.
  - So only SN (app t1 t2) is problematic.

For SN (app t1 t2), let's try the nested induction on SN t1 and SN t2 with the simple T definition:
- Reducts: App1 → app t1' t2: apply IH on SN t1 with T (T1->T2) t1' (closure) and T T1 t2. Need the outer induction motive: P x := T (T1->T2) x -> forall t2, T T1 t2 -> SN (app x t2)? But the induction is on SN t1, and P x for reducts x. However, in the App1 case we need T (T1->T2) t1' — closure gives it. Good.
- App2 → app t1 t2' with t1 value, t2 --> t2': need an inner induction on SN t2 (motive Q y := T T1 y -> SN (app t1 y)) — closure gives T T1 t2'. Good.
- beta → open body t2 with t1 = abs T body, t2 value: Need SN (open body t2). Hmm. What do we know? T (T1->T2) t1 with t1 a value and t1 -->* t1 gives V (T1->T2) t1 = forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app t1 u); apply to t2: T T2 (app t1 t2) — circular (its SN component is what we're proving).
  
  BUT: maybe we can avoid circularity by proving SN (open body t2) via a *different* lemma: the general "SN of an abstraction applied to a related value" needs the body semantics. But T (T1->T2)(abs body) doesn't reveal the body semantics... 
  
  Hmm, is there any way to get SN (open body t2) from SN (app (abs body) t2)? That would be circular since we're proving SN of the app.

  Wait, actually, we could prove SN (app t1 t2) by strong induction on the *SN derivation of t1* and t2, where the beta case uses... no.

Hmm, alternatively: maybe prove SN (app t1 t2) by using the T_app_abs-style lemma where the hypothesis Hbody is derived from T (T1->T2) t1? But T (T1->T2) t1 doesn't give Hbody (it gives V on value reducts).

So indeed, T needs to explicitly include "SN of applications" for arrow types, as I concluded. Let's go with:

```
Fixpoint T (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  SN t /\ lc t /\
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      (forall u, T T1 u -> SN (tm_app t u)) /\
      (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> V Ty v)
  end
with ... 
```
where V Ty v := match Ty with Bool => True | Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u) end.

Now T (T1->T2) t1 gives SN (app t1 t2) directly for T T1 t2. So the T_App SN component is immediate. 

And T_Abs requires:
(a) forall u, T T1 u -> SN (app (abs) u): SN_app_abs. Beta case: SN (open body' v) for values v ∈ T T1 from Hbody v : T T2 (open body' v) → T_sn.
(b) forall value reducts v of abs: V (T1->T2) v = forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app abs u) [since v = abs]. This is the T_app_abs lemma:
    - SN (app abs u): from (a).
    - lc.
    - If T2 = arrow: (i) forall u', T T2a u' -> SN (app (app abs u) u') and (ii) forall value reducts v of app abs u, V T2 v.
      (i): This is the nested application SN problem again! Hmm. But now note: T (T1->T2) (app abs u) is what we're proving, and it requires forall u' ∈ T T2a, SN (app (app abs u) u'). 
      
      How to prove SN (app (app abs u) u')? Use the general lemma: if SN f and for all value reducts v_f of f, SN (app v_f u'), then SN (app f u')? Let's prove that lemma (with SN f): 
      
      **Lemma SN_app_of_SN**: forall f w, SN f -> SN w -> (forall v, f -->* v -> value v -> SN (app v w)) -> SN (app f w).

      Let me prove the specialized lemma directly:
      ```
      Lemma app_abs_multistep_value : forall T body u v,
        multi step (app (abs T body) u) v -> value v ->
        exists v2, multi step u v2 /\ value v2 /\ multi step (open body v2) v.
      Proof.
        intros T body u v Hmulti. induction Hmulti as [| x y z Hstep Hmulti IH]; intros Hv.
        - (* multi_refl: app (abs) u = v, value v *) 
          exfalso. apply value_app_abs in ... (* value (app ...) is impossible *)
        - (* step app... x, then x -->* z *)
          inversion Hstep; subst.
          + (* AppAbs: x = open body v0, u = v0, value v0 *) 
            exists v0. split; [apply multi_refl|]. split; [assumption|]. assumption.  (* the tail x -->* z is open body v0 -->* z *)
          + (* App2: value v1 where v1 = abs T body; u --> u'; x = app (abs T body) u' *)
            destruct (IH Hv) as [v2 [Hu' [Hval Hbody]]]. exists v2. split.
            * eapply multi_step; [exact H|exact Hu'].
            * split; assumption.
          + (* App1: abs T body --> t1'; contradiction *) 
            exfalso. (* need lemma about abs not stepping *) ...
      ```
      Note: inversion Hstep will need care because the term `app (abs T body) u` unifies with `tm_app t1 t2`.       For the App1 case: hypothesis `tm_abs T body --> t1'`; we can prove `abs_no_step`.       Similarly for the App2 case, the hypothesis `value v1` where v1 = tm_abs T body is used for the "requires value" but we don't need it since we don't need lc for the step. Actually ST_App2's hypotheses: value v1 and t2 --> t2'. We use t2 --> t2' (u --> u') for the multistep. Good.

Now T_app_abs's proof in detail:
Given: A := T1, B := T2, body := body', Hlc : lc (abs A body), Hbody : forall v, T A v -> T B (open body v).
Goal: forall u, T A u -> T B (app (abs A body) u). (Here "app (abs A body) u" is the term.)
Proof: intros u Hu.
Destruct B as [| B1 B2].
- B = Bool: T Bool x = SN x ∧ lc x.
  - SN: apply SN_app_abs with Hlc and (forall v, T A v -> SN (open body v)) [from Hbody + T_sn]; Hu.
  - lc: lc_app (lc_abs Hlc) (T_lc Hu).
- B = B1 -> B2: T (B1->B2) x = SN x ∧ lc x ∧ (forall w, T B1 w -> SN (app x w)) ∧ (forall v, x -->* v -> value v -> V (B1->B2) v).
  - SN: SN_app_abs.
  - lc: lc_app.
  - forall w, T B1 w -> SN (app (app abs u) w): use SN_app_of_value_reds_R with f := app (abs A body) u, R := T B1, w; SN f (previous), R w, and the condition: forall v, f -->* v -> value v -> forall x, T B1 x -> SN (app v x).
    Proof of condition: given f -->* v, value v, x with T B1 x.
      By app_abs_multistep_value (with the multistep f -->* v): exists v2, u -->* v2, value v2, open body v2 -->* v.
      Hbody v2 (T_multistep Hu Hu2) : T (B1->B2) (open body v2). Its SN-app conjunct: forall x, T B1 x -> SN (app (open body v2) x). Apply to x: SN (app (open body v2) x).
      Now multi_app_l: open body v2 -->* v, lc x (T_lc Hx) ⟹ app (open body v2) x -->* app v x.
      SN_multistep gives SN (app v x). ✓
  - forall v, (app abs u) -->* v -> value v -> V (B1->B2) v = forall w, T B1 w -> T (B1->B2)??? 
    Hold on: V (B1->B2) v is defined as forall w, T B1 w -> T B2 (app v w). Yes (B2 here is the codomain of B = B1->B2, i.e., the original T2's second component). Wait I need to be careful with naming. Let me rename: T2 = Arrow B1 B2. Then:
    - T (Arrow B1 B2) x = SN x ∧ lc x ∧ (forall w, T B1 w -> SN (app x w)) ∧ (forall v, x -->* v -> value v -> V (Arrow B1 B2) v).
    - V (Arrow B1 B2) v = forall w, T B1 w -> T B2 (app v w).
    So the value-reduct conjunct needs V T2 v = forall w, T B1 w -> T B2 (app v w).
    Proof: given f -->* v, value v, w with T B1 w. Decompose f -->* v: exists v2, u -->* v2, value v2, open body v2 -->* v. Hbody v2 (T_multistep Hu Hu2) : T (Arrow B1 B2) (open body v2). Its value-reduct conjunct gives V (Arrow B1 B2) v (since open body v2 -->* v and value v): forall w, T B1 w -> T B2 (app v w). Apply to w. ✓
    
    So V T2's definition uses T B2 where B2 is a subterm of T2 = Arrow B1 B2 — smaller type. And Hbody is at type T2. Good.

Now SN_app_abs:
```
Lemma SN_app_abs : forall A body u,
  lc (abs A body) ->
  (forall v, T A v -> SN (open body v)) ->
  T A u ->
  SN (app (abs A body) u).
Proof.
  intros A body u Hlc Hbody Hu.
  destruct Hu as [Hsn Hu_lc Hu_rest]? (* T A u's structure depends on A *)
```
Hmm, T A u's first component is SN u. We need induction on that SN derivation. We can do: `destruct (T_sn Hu) as ...`; better: `remember (T_sn Hu) as Hsn`? The standard way:
```
  assert (Hsn : SN u) by apply T_sn; assumption.
  revert Hu.
  induction Hsn as [u Hred IH].
  intros Hu.
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  inversion Hstep; subst.
  - (* AppAbs: t' = open body u0 where u = u0 and value u0 *)
    apply Hbody. assumption. (* need T A u where u is the value; we have Hu : T A u *)
  - (* App2: value v1 = abs; u --> u'; t' = app (abs) u' *)
    apply IH. assumption. (* Hred gives SN u' and IH : forall u', u --> u' -> T A u' -> SN (app ... u') *)
  - (* App1: abs --> t1'; contradiction *)
    inversion H. (* or value_no_step *)
Qed.
```
Wait: in the AppAbs case, we need `Hbody u` which requires T A u; we have Hu. But careful: Hbody's hypothesis is `T A v -> SN (open body v)` and in the AppAbs case, v = u (the argument), and value u comes from the step rule but not needed for Hbody. We have Hu : T A u. So `apply Hbody; exact Hu`. Good.
In the App2 case, the IH from `induction Hsn` gives: forall u', u --> u' -> T A u' -> SN (app (abs A body) u'). We need T A u' from T A u and u --> u' — T_step. Good.
In the App1 case: the step's head is `abs A body --> t1'`; inversion should find no constructor. But wait, `inversion Hstep` on the whole step will do case analysis; in the App1 case, the hypothesis `tm_abs A body --> t1'` remains; `inversion` of that sub-hypothesis closes it. We can use `match goal with H : tm_abs _ _ --> _ |- _ => inversion H end` or name it.

Hmm, wait: `inversion Hstep` requires Hstep : step (app (abs A body) u) t'. The constructors:
- ST_AppAbs T t v: lc(abs T t), value v, app (abs T t) v = app (abs A body) u — inversion will equate T = A, t = body, v = u, t' = open body u.
- ST_App1 t1 t1' t2: t1 --> t1', lc t2, app t1 t2 = app (abs A body) u — so t1 = abs A body, t2 = u, t' = app t1' u.
- ST_App2 v1 t2 t2': value v1, t2 --> t2', app v1 t2 = app (abs A body) u — v1 = abs A body, t2 = u, t' = app (abs A body) t2'.
Inversion should work but might generate heterogeneous equalities; typically `inversion Hstep; subst` handles it if the terms are literally the same. To help, we might use `inversion Hstep as [...]` or pattern. We'll see.

Now SN_app_of_value_reds_R:
```
Lemma SN_app_of_value_reds : forall (R : tm -> Prop) f w,
  (forall x y, R x -> step x y -> R y) ->
  SN f -> R w ->
  (forall v, multi step f v -> value v -> forall x, R x -> SN (app v x)) ->
  SN (app f w).
Proof.
  intros R f w Rstep Hsn HRw Hcond.
  induction Hsn as [f Hred IH].
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  inversion Hstep; subst.
  - (* AppAbs: f = abs T body, w value, t' = open body w *) 
    (* Hcond f (refl) value f w HRw : SN (app f w); then SN t' *)
    ...
  - (* App1: f --> f'; t' = app f' w *)
    apply IH; [assumption | ... condition for f' ...].
  - (* App2: value f, w --> w'; t' = app f w' *)
    (* Hcond f (refl) (value f) w' (Rstep w w') *)
```
Hmm, in the AppAbs case, the condition `Hcond f (multi_refl f) (value f)` requires `value f` — in AppAbs, f = abs T body and the ST_AppAbs premise gives lc(abs T body), so value f. Then Hcond gives SN (app f w). But t' = open body w. We need SN t'. Since SN (app f w) and app f w --> t' (the step), SN t' follows from SN_intro's premise. Good: `apply (SN_inv ...)`? We can prove a lemma `SN_step : SN t -> step t t' -> SN t'` by inversion on SN t. Let me prove:
```
Lemma SN_step : forall t t', SN t -> step t t' -> SN t'.
Proof. intros t t' Hsn Hstep. inversion Hsn; subst. apply H0; assumption. Qed.
```
Wait, `inversion Hsn` with Hsn : SN t and Hstep : step t t' — the SN_intro gives premise forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'. Applying to the step gives SN t'. But inversion on Hsn gives the premise `forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'` as H. Then `apply H; exact Hstep`. Yes.

Then in AppAbs: `apply SN_step with (t := app f w); [apply Hcond ... | exact Hstep]`. Wait, we need SN (app f w) from Hcond: Hcond f (multi_refl f) Hval w HRw. Then SN_step.

Hmm, but actually in the AppAbs case we could also just apply Hcond and SN_step. Fine.

In the App2 case: t' = app f w' with value f and w --> w'. Hcond f (refl) Hval w' (Rstep w w' HRw) gives SN (app f w'). ✓.

In the App1 case: t' = app f' w with f --> f'. Apply IH f' Hstep with R w and the condition for f': forall v, f' -->* v -> value v -> forall x, R x -> SN (app v x). Given f' -->* v, we get f -->* v via multi_step, then Hcond. ✓.

Now let's also double check T_step (closure of T under step). We'll need it for T_multistep in several places.
```
Lemma T_step : forall Ty t t', T Ty t -> step t t' -> T Ty t'.
Proof.
  intros Ty. destruct Ty as [| T1 T2]; intros t t' HT Hstep.
  - (* Bool *) destruct HT as [Hsn Hlc]. split.
    + apply SN_step with t; assumption.
    + (* lc preservation *) apply lc_step; assumption.
  - (* Arrow *) 
    destruct HT as [Hsn [Hlc [Happ Hval]]]. split; [|split].
    + apply SN_step with t; assumption.
    + apply lc_step; assumption.
    + (* forall u, T T1 u -> SN (app t' u) *)
      intros u Hu. 
      (* We have SN (app t u) from Happ u Hu. 
         Need SN (app t' u). Do we have a step app t u --> app t' u? Requires lc u: yes T_lc Hu. 
         So SN_step (app t u) (app t' u). *)
      apply SN_step with (tm_app t u); [apply Happ; assumption | apply ST_App1; assumption].
    + (* forall v, t' -->* v -> value v -> V Ty v *)
      intros v Hmulti Hv. apply Hval with v.
      * apply multi_step with t'; [exact Hstep | exact Hmulti].
      * assumption.
Qed.
```
Wait: the third component: `SN (app t' u)` from `SN (app t u)` and `app t u --> app t' u`. ST_App1 requires `t --> t'` and `locally_closed t2` where t2 = u; T_lc Hu : lc u. ✓.

And T_multistep by induction on the multistep.

Proof. intros Ty; destruct Ty; simpl; intros t H; apply H. Qed. 
```
Hmm, `destruct Ty; simpl; intros t H; exact (proj1 H)`? For Bool: T Bool t = SN t ∧ lc t... after simpl, `T Ty_Bool t` might not reduce if T is a Fixpoint on Ty and Ty is a constructor — it should reduce by iota. Let me define T carefully so that `simpl` unfolds it. Actually if T is:
```
Fixpoint T (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  SN t /\ lc t /\
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => (forall u, T T1 u -> SN (tm_app t u)) /\ (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> V Ty v)
  end.
```
But V is defined after T. Hmm, ordering: T's definition mentions V Ty v (in the arrow branch), and V mentions T. If V is defined after T as a Definition using T, Coq allows T's definition to mention V only if V is already defined. We have a circular dependency. Options:
(a) Inline the V formula in T's definition (so T doesn't mention V), then define V afterward as the same match. Then T_arrow's value-reduct conjunct is `forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u)` (inlined).
(b) Define V first as a Fixpoint that mentions T — impossible.
(c) Define T and V via a single Fixpoint returning a pair of predicates? Let's think: 

Actually, maybe we can define V *before* T by making V call T on smaller types: V (Arrow T1 T2) v = forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u) — T is not yet defined. So no.

Option (a): inline. Let's write:
```
Fixpoint T (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  SN t /\
  lc t /\
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      (forall u : tm, T T1 u -> strongly_normalizing (tm_app t u)) /\
      (forall v : tm, multi step t v -> value v ->
         forall u : tm, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u))
  end.
```
Then:
```
Definition V (Ty : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u : tm, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u)
  end.
```
Guard condition: T's recursive calls are T T1 u, T T2 (app v u) where T1, T2 are subterms of Ty in the Arrow branch. Coq's guard checker should accept this since the match is on Ty and the recursive calls are on the variables bound by the pattern. Yes, this is standard.

Now, let's think about the subst lemma and the composition lemma definitions.

```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.
```
Note: locally nameless, so no shifting.

`free_atom x t`:
```
Fixpoint free_atom (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => free_atom x t1 \/ free_atom x t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => free_atom x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  end.
```
And `fv t : list atom` for freshness selection:
```
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  end.
```
And a lemma: `In x (fv t) <-> free_atom x t`.

Composition lemma:
```
Lemma subst_open_fvar : forall sigma x v t,
  ~ free_atom x t ->
  subst (update sigma x v) (open t (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t) v.
```
Hmm, what's the type of `update`? `update : context -> atom -> ty -> context`, i.e., only for contexts! For sigma : atom -> tm, we need a separate update-like function on term environments:
```
Definition extend (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.
```
Then the lemma:
```
Lemma subst_open_fvar : forall sigma x v t,
  ~ free_atom x t ->
  subst (extend sigma x v) (open t (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t) v.
```
Proof by induction on t, generalized over the opening depth. Since `open t (fvar x) = open_rec 0 ...` and under binders the depth increases, we need a generalized statement:
```
Lemma subst_open_rec_fvar : forall k sigma x v t,
  ~ free_atom x t ->
  subst (extend sigma x v) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k v (subst sigma t).
```
Is this true? Let's check the bvar case: t = tm_bvar i. LHS: subst (extend) (if i =? k then fvar x else bvar i) = if i =? k then (extend sigma x v) x = v else bvar i. RHS: open_rec k v (subst sigma (bvar i)) = open_rec k v (bvar i) = if i =? k then v else bvar i. ✓.
- fvar y: t = fvar y, fresh means x ≠ y. LHS: subst (extend) (fvar y) = extend sigma x y = sigma y (since y ≠ x). RHS: open_rec k v (subst sigma (fvar y)) = open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y (open_rec k v doesn't touch fvars). ✓.
- app: straightforward by IH.
- abs T t1: LHS: subst (extend) (open_rec k (fvar x) (abs T t1)) = subst (extend) (abs T (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)) = abs T (subst (extend) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) v (subst sigma t1)) by IH with k := S k. RHS: open_rec k v (subst sigma (abs T t1)) = open_rec k v (abs T (subst sigma t1)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) v (subst sigma t1)). ✓.
- true/false ✓.
Great, the generalized lemma holds.

Then the T_Abs case uses it with k=0: subst (extend sigma x v) (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) v, given x not free in t1.

But wait, in the T_Abs case, the freshness we have from typing is `~ In x L`; we need `~ free_atom x t1` too. We choose x fresh from L ∪ fv(t1). ✓.

Also, the environment extension: in the T_Abs case we set sigma' = extend sigma x v as a term environment. And the context update is update Gamma x T1. The env_sat condition for sigma' w.r.t. update Gamma x T1: as discussed. Note update uses `if Nat.eqb x y` and extend uses `if Nat.eqb x y` too (same x first). Good: `extend sigma x v y = if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y` and `update Gamma x T1 y = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T1 else Gamma y`. Consistent.

Let me now also double check: in the T_Abs case, `sem_ty Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)` and `subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1) = tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)`. ✓.

Now, another crucial check: the fundamental theorem statement and the "empty context" instantiation. The theorem: `forall t T, has_type empty t T -> strongly_normalizing t`. Let me think about the exact Coq syntax for the Fixpoint T with the recursive calls under a match. 

```
Fixpoint T (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  locally_closed t /\
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      (forall u : tm, T T1 u -> strongly_normalizing (tm_app t u)) /\
      (forall v : tm, multi step t v -> value v ->
         forall u : tm, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u))
  end.
```

Will the guard checker accept this? The recursive calls T T1 u and T T2 (tm_app v u) appear in the branch of a match on Ty where T1 and T2 are pattern variables of the constructor Ty_Arrow T1 T2. Yes, this is the standard "recursive function on an inductive with match" — guard condition accepts calls on variables bound in the pattern of the match on the decreasing argument. Should be fine.

But there might be an issue: the recursive occurrences appear in the *type* of a forall, i.e., in `T T1 u -> strongly_normalizing (tm_app t u)` — this is a negative occurrence? For Fixpoints, there's no positivity requirement, only the guard condition. Fine.

Now, defining V after T:
```
Definition V (Ty : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u : tm, T T1 u -> T T2 (tm_app v u)
  end.
```

First, some basic list/nat fresh stuff:
```
Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma max_list_ge : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L Hin. unfold fresh in Hin. apply max_list_ge in Hin.
  apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l in ... 
```
Hmm, `S (max_list L) <= max_list L` is false. `Nat.nle_succ_diag_l : forall n, ~ S n <= n`. So:
```

  apply (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l (max_list L) H).
```
Wait, `Nat.nle_succ_diag_l : forall n : nat, ~ S n <= n`. So `exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l (max_list L) H)`.

Now, free_atom and fv:
```
Fixpoint free_atom (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => free_atom x t1 \/ free_atom x t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => free_atom x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  end.

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar y => [y]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  end.

Lemma fv_spec : forall t x, In x (fv t) <-> free_atom x t.
Proof. induction t; simpl; try (split; intros H; auto; fail).
...
```
Let's do it carefully:
- bvar n: In x [] ↔ False: `split; [intros [] | intros []]`.
- fvar a: In x [a] ↔ x = a: `split; intros H; [now left-inversion | subst; left; reflexivity]`. Actually `simpl. split; intros H. - destruct H as [-> | []]. reflexivity. - subst; left; reflexivity.` Hmm `In x [a]` unfolds to `x = a \/ In x []`. Let me write: `simpl. split; intros H; [destruct H as [-> | []] | subst; left; reflexivity]`.
- app: In x (fv t1 ++ fv t2) ↔ In x (fv t1) \/ In x (fv t2): `apply in_app_iff`. Then rewrite IHs.
- abs: same as body.
- true/false: In x [] ↔ False.

Lemma: `fresh_fv : ~ free_atom (fresh (L ++ fv t)) t`. Using fresh_notin and fv_spec: fresh L' ∉ L' so not in fv t; by fv_spec, ¬ free_atom. But we need `~ In (fresh (L ++ fv t)) (fv t)`: from ~ In ... (L ++ fv t) and In x (fv t) → In x (L ++ fv t) via in_or_app (right). ✓.

Careful: `Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m, n <> m -> (n =? m) = false`. Let me check the exact lemma name: In PeanoNat, `Nat.eqb_eq : forall n m, (n =? m) = true <-> n = m` and `Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m, (n =? m) = false <-> n <> m`. So `apply Nat.eqb_neq; exact H` might work with the iff. Or use `rewrite Nat.eqb_neq by assumption`. Let me use:
```
Lemma update_neq : forall Gamma x T y, x <> y -> update Gamma x T y = Gamma y.
Proof. intros. unfold update. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.
```
Hmm, `Nat.eqb_neq` as an iff: `(n =? m) = false <-> n <> m`. `apply Nat.eqb_neq in H` where H : x <> y gives `(x =? y) = false`. That should work if the direction is right (iff, so `apply ... in H` uses the reverse direction: from n <> m to (n =? m) = false). Since it's an iff, `apply Nat.eqb_neq in H` will work if the lemma's conclusion matches the goal direction; with iff, Coq should pick the appropriate direction. Apply IH Hv T body t2' eq_refl: get v2 with u' = t2' -->* v2, value v2, open body v2 -->* z. Need multi step u v2: from u --> t2' -->* v2 via multi_step Hstep2. ✓

Fine.

Lemma multi_app_l : forall t1 t1' t2,
  multi step t1 t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> multi step (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2).

Actually simplest: induction on the derivation of multi step t1 t1', with motive P a b := lc t2 -> multi step (app a t2) (app b t2) and t2 universally quantified. To handle the revert, do:
```
intros t1 t1' t2 Hm. revert t1' Hm.

```
Lemma multi_app_l : forall t1 t1', multi step t1 t1' -> forall t2, locally_closed t2 -> multi step (tm_app t1 t2) (tm_app t1' t2).
Proof.
  intros t1 t1' Hm.
  induction Hm as [x | x y z Hstep Hm IH].
  - intros t2 Hlc. apply multi_refl.
  - intros t2 Hlc. apply multi_step with (tm_app y t2).
    + apply ST_App1; assumption.
    + apply IH. exact Hlc.
Qed.
```
Here the goal after `intros t1 t1' Hm` is `forall t2, lc t2 -> multi ...`. The induction on Hm generalizes t1 t1' (variables) — but the goal mentions them; Coq abstracts. The statement becomes P a b := forall t2, lc t2 -> multi (app a t2) (app b t2)? Hmm, `induction Hm` where the goal is `forall t2, ...` with t1, t1' free variables: Coq will generalize the goal over t1 and t1' (since they're indices of Hm) and produce the motive. The hypothesis Hm gets consumed. The refl case: goal `forall t2, lc t2 -> multi (app x t2) (app x t2)` where x is the common start/end. ✓ apply multi_refl. The step case: x --> y, multi y z, IH : forall t2, lc t2 -> multi (app y t2) (app z t2); goal forall t2, lc t2 -> multi (app x t2) (app z t2). Use multi_step with app y t2: ST_App1 Hstep Hlc, then IH Hlc. ✓

Good, this pattern works (the induction abstracts the variables automatically).

- T_Var: lc_fvar.
- T_Abs: need lc (abs T1 t1) = lc_at 0 (abs T1 t1). apply lc_abs. Need lc_at 1 t1. Choose x := fresh (L ++ fv t1). Have Hx : ~ In x L (from fresh not in L ++ fv) and Hfx : ~ free_atom x t1 (from fv_spec + fresh).

```
Actually fresh_notin gives it directly. Then:

Now the composition lemma:
```
Lemma subst_open_rec_fvar : forall t k sigma x v,
  ~ free_atom x t ->
  subst (extend sigma x v) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k v (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  induction t as [i | y | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k sigma x v Hfresh; simpl.
```
Hmm, `simpl` might not unfold open_rec and subst appropriately; let's do it case by case.
- bvar i: need to show `subst (extend sigma x v) (if i =? k then fvar x else bvar i) = open_rec k v (subst sigma (bvar i))`. Note subst sigma (bvar i) = bvar i and open_rec k v (bvar i) = if i =? k then v else bvar i. So: destruct (Nat.eqb i k); simpl; [apply extend_eq | reflexivity].
  Careful: `subst (extend sigma x v) (tm_fvar x)` = `extend sigma x v x` = v via extend_eq. ✓
- fvar y: Hfresh : ~ free_atom x (fvar y) → x ≠ y. `subst (extend sigma x v) (tm_fvar y)` = extend sigma x v y = sigma y (since y ≠ x; need x <> y from x ≠ y). RHS: open_rec k v (subst sigma (fvar y)) = open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y (open_rec doesn't touch fvars; but is that definitional? open_rec on an arbitrary term (sigma y) may not simplify since sigma y is not a constructor! We need a lemma: `open_rec_fvar : forall k u t, ...` hmm, open_rec k v (sigma y) where sigma y is an arbitrary term: open_rec will match on it but it's a variable term; it doesn't reduce. So RHS = open_rec k v (sigma y), and LHS = sigma y. These aren't syntactically equal!
  
  Hmm, this breaks the lemma as stated. The RHS has `open_rec k v (subst sigma t)`; for t = fvar y, subst sigma t = sigma y, and open_rec k v (sigma y) is not sigma y definitionally (only propositionally, by a lemma `open_rec_no_bvar` if sigma y has no free bvar k... but it's not definitional).
  
  So the lemma needs to be stated differently, or we need to distribute the opening before substitution. Standard approach: prove that opening a substituted term equals substituting an opened term when the opened variable doesn't occur: `open_rec k v (subst sigma t) = subst sigma' (open_rec k (fvar x) t)` — the RHS opens first. In the fvar y case: LHS = open_rec k v (sigma y), RHS = subst (extend sigma x v) (fvar y) = sigma y. Not equal unless open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y. Hmm, so we need `open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y` — is that true? Not if sigma y contains bvar k (as a free variable). But if sigma y is locally closed (no bvars), then yes by a lemma. Hmm! In our use case, sigma maps to locally closed terms. But the composition lemma would then require `lc (sigma y)` for relevant y.

  Alternative: change the direction and definition. Perhaps define the composition differently: instead of `open (subst sigma body) v`, we can arrange the body semantics to directly produce the right form. Let's reconsider: the IH gives `T T2 (subst (extend sigma x v) (open body (fvar x)))`. We want `T T2 (open (subst sigma body) v)`. The LHS is "open first with fvar x, then substitute"; RHS is "substitute first, then open with v". 
  
  Let's prove the equality `subst (extend sigma x v) (open_rec k (fvar x) t) = open_rec k v (subst sigma t)` under the hypothesis that sigma maps to locally closed terms and x is fresh in t. In the fvar y case: LHS = sigma y; RHS = open_rec k v (sigma y). If sigma y is locally closed (lc_at 0), then since it has no bvars, open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y. Need a lemma: `lc_at 0 u -> open_rec k v u = u`? Hmm, if u has no bvars, opening does nothing: `open_rec_no_bvar : (forall i, ~ free_bvar i u) -> open_rec k v u = u`. We can prove `open_rec_id : lc_at 0 u -> forall k v, open_rec k v u = u`. Since lc_at 0 u means no bvars, and open_rec only replaces bvars. Prove by induction on u.
  
  Hmm, but under binders, we need `open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y` for any k, which follows from lc_at 0 (sigma y) and the no-bvar lemma. So the composition lemma should assume `forall y, lc_at 0 (sigma y)`. That's exactly the env_sat lc condition! Great.

  So state:
  ```
  Lemma subst_open_rec_fvar : forall t k sigma x v,
    ~ free_atom x t ->
    (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
    subst (extend sigma x v) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k v (subst sigma t).
  ```
  Let's recheck all cases with the `open_rec_id` lemma:
  - fvar y: LHS = sigma y (since x ≠ y); RHS = open_rec k v (sigma y) = sigma y by open_rec_id (lc_at 0 (sigma y)). ✓
  - bvar i: ✓ as before (RHS: subst sigma (bvar i) = bvar i, then open_rec k v (bvar i) = if i=?k then v else bvar i; LHS similarly with extend sigma x v x = v). ✓
  - app: IH1, IH2. LHS: subst (...) (app (open_rec k (fvar x) t1) (open_rec k (fvar x) t2)) = app (subst ...) (subst ...). RHS: open_rec k v (app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)) = app (open_rec k v (subst sigma t1)) (open_rec k v (subst sigma t2)). Rewrite IHs ✓.
  - abs T t1: IH with k := S k. LHS: subst (...) (abs T (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)) = abs T (subst (...) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) v (subst sigma t1)) by IH. RHS: open_rec k v (abs T (subst sigma t1)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) v (subst sigma t1)). ✓
  - true/false ✓.
  
  Great. And in the T_Abs case, we have env_sat sigma Gamma which gives forall y, lc (sigma y). ✓

Now, about `subst_lc`: it requires `forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)` and uses monotonicity. Let's write:
```
Lemma lc_at_subst : forall t n sigma,
  lc_at n t -> (forall x, lc_at 0 (sigma x)) -> lc_at n (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  induction t; intros n sigma Hlc Hsig; simpl.
  - (* bvar *) inversion Hlc; subst; apply lc_bvar; assumption.
  - (* fvar *) apply lc_at_mono with 0; [lia | apply Hsig].
  - (* app *) inversion Hlc; subst; apply lc_app; [apply IHt1; assumption | apply IHt2; assumption].
  - (* abs *) inversion Hlc; subst; apply lc_abs; apply IHt; [assumption | assumption].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```
Hmm, the induction on t generalizing n sigma: `induction t; intros n sigma Hlc Hsig; simpl`. In the abs case, Hlc : lc_at n (abs T t1); inversion gives lc_at (S n) t1; goal lc_at n (abs T (subst sigma t1)) → apply lc_abs → lc_at (S n) (subst sigma t1); apply IHt with (S n) sigma. ✓

Note: `simpl` on subst: `subst sigma (tm_bvar i)` = tm_bvar i; the goal for bvar becomes `lc_at n (tm_bvar i)` given Hlc : lc_at n (tm_bvar i) ✓ (after inversion we have i < n).

```
Lemma T_lc : forall Ty t, T Ty t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros Ty t H. destruct Ty; simpl in H; exact (proj1 (proj2 H)). Qed.
```
For Bool: H : SN t ∧ lc t ∧ True; proj2 H : lc t ∧ True; proj1 : lc t ✓.
For Arrow: H : SN t ∧ lc t ∧ (A ∧ B); proj2 H : lc t ∧ (A ∧ B); proj1 ✓.

```
Lemma T_app_SN : forall T1 T2 t u, T (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> T T1 u -> strongly_normalizing (tm_app t u).

```
Lemma T_value_red : forall Ty t v, T Ty t -> multi step t v -> value v -> V Ty v.
Proof.
  intros Ty t v H Hm Hv. destruct Ty as [| T1 T2]; simpl in *.

  - (* Arrow *) exact (proj2 (proj2 (proj2 H)) v Hm Hv).
Qed.
```

For Arrow, `V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v` should reduce (by simpl) to `forall u, T T1 u -> T T2 (app v u)`, and `proj2 (proj2 (proj2 H)) v Hm Hv` has exactly that type. ```
Lemma T_step : forall Ty t t', T Ty t -> step t t' -> T Ty t'.
Proof.
  intros Ty t t' H Hstep. destruct Ty as [| T1 T2]; simpl in *.
  - (* Bool *) destruct H as [Hsn Hlc]. split.
    + apply SN_step with t; assumption.
    + apply lc_step with t; assumption.
  - (* Arrow *) destruct H as [Hsn [Hlc [Happ Hval]]]. split; [| split].
    + apply SN_step with t; assumption.
    + apply lc_step with t; assumption.
    + intros u Hu. apply SN_step with (tm_app t u).
      * apply Happ; assumption.
      * apply ST_App1; [assumption | apply T_lc with (Ty := T1); assumption].
    + intros v Hm Hv. apply Hval with v.
      * apply multi_step with t'; [assumption | assumption].
      * assumption.
Qed.
```
Careful with the destruct patterns: for Bool, H after simpl : SN t ∧ lc t ∧ True; `destruct H as [Hsn Hlc]` gives Hlc : lc t ∧ True, so `apply lc_step with t` expects locally_closed t but Hlc is lc t ∧ True. Hmm, need `destruct H as [Hsn [Hlc _]]`. Let's use that.
For Arrow: H : SN t ∧ lc t ∧ (A ∧ B) → `destruct H as [Hsn [Hlc [Happ Hval]]]`. ✓
`apply lc_step with t` — lc_step : forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'. Applying `lc_step with t` gives goal `step t t'` and `locally_closed t`? Actually `apply lc_step with t` unifies t and t' with the goal `locally_closed t'`; the remaining goals are `step t t'` and `lc t`. Let's write `apply (lc_step t t' Hstep Hlc)` explicitly. Hmm, lc_step's argument order: `forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'`. So `lc_step t t' Hstep Hlc`. Let me define lc_step with step first as written.

Now SN_app_abs:
```
Lemma SN_app_abs : forall A body u,
  (forall v, T A v -> strongly_normalizing (open body v)) ->
  T A u ->
  strongly_normalizing (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u).
Proof.
  intros A body u Hbody Hu.
  assert (Hsn : strongly_normalizing u) by apply T_sn with A; exact Hu.
  revert Hu.
  induction Hsn as [u0 Hred IH].
  intros Hu.
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  inversion Hstep as [T0 t0 v0 Hlc0 Hval0 | t1 t1' t2 Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval1 Hstep2]; subst.
```

```
Lemma T_app_abs : forall A B body,
  locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
  (forall v, T A v -> T B (open body v)) ->
  forall u, T A u -> T B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u).
Proof.
  intros A B body Hlc Hbody u Hu.
  destruct B as [| B1 B2].
  - (* B = Bool *) simpl. split.
    + (* SN *) apply SN_app_abs with (A := A) (body := body).
      * intros v Hv. apply T_sn with B. apply Hbody. exact Hv.
      * exact Hu.
    + (* lc *) apply lc_app. * exact Hlc. * apply T_lc with A; exact Hu.
  - (* B = B1 -> B2 *) simpl. split; [| split].
    + apply SN_app_abs with A body; [intros v Hv; apply T_sn with (Ty_Arrow B1 B2); apply Hbody; exact Hv | exact Hu].
    + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply T_lc with A; exact Hu].
    + (* forall w, T B1 w -> SN (app (app abs u) w) *)
      intros w Hw.
      apply SN_app_of_value_reds with (R := T B1) (f := tm_app (tm_abs A body) u) (w := w).
      * (* R step-closed *) intros x y Hx Hs. apply T_step with x; assumption.
      * (* SN f *) [the first conjunct we just proved]
      * exact Hw.
      * (* condition *) intros v Hm Hval x Hx.
        (* v is a value reduct of app abs u *)
        destruct (multi_app_abs_decomp ...) ...
```

Now, the fundamental theorem:
```
Definition env_sat (sigma : atom -> tm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> T T (sigma x)).

Definition sem_ty (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (Ty : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma, env_sat sigma Gamma -> T Ty (subst sigma t).

Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t Ty, has_type Gamma t Ty -> sem_ty Gamma t Ty.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t Ty H. induction H as [Gamma x T Hx | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma].
  - (* T_Var *) intros sigma [Hlc Hg]. simpl. apply Hg. exact Hx.
  - (* T_Abs *) intros sigma [Hlc_sigma Hsat].
    simpl. (* subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1) = tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1) *)
    (* choose x fresh *)
    set (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).
    assert (HnotL : ~ In x L) by ...
    assert (Hnotfree : ~ free_atom x t1) by ...
    assert (HIH : T T2 (subst (extend sigma x (sigma x? no v)) ...))
```

- SN: SN_abs.
- lc: lc_app? no, lc(abs T1 (subst sigma t1)): apply lc_abs; need lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). From has_type_lc on the abstraction: locally_closed (tm_abs T1 t1) = lc_at 1 t1; then lc_at_subst with n=1 using Hlc_sigma. ✓

Fixpoint TermRel (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  locally_closed t /\
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      (forall u : tm, TermRel T1 u -> strongly_normalizing (tm_app t u)) /\
      (forall v : tm, multi step t v -> value v ->
         forall u : tm, TermRel T1 u -> TermRel T2 (tm_app v u))
  end.

Definition ValRel (Ty : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=

  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u : tm, TermRel T1 u -> TermRel T2 (tm_app v u)
  end.
```

```coq
(** ... original header ... *)

From Stdlib Require Import Arith.PeanoNat.
From Stdlib Require Import Lists.List.
From Stdlib Require Import Relations.Relation_Definitions.
From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

Module STLCNormalizationNoneHardTask.

... original definitions ...

(** Logical relation development. *)

(* ---------- Fresh atoms ---------- *)

Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | cons x xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma max_list_ge : forall (L : list nat) (x : nat), In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl in Hin. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list L); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_ge L (S (max_list L)) Hin).
  exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l (max_list L) H).
Qed.
```
Wait, `Nat.nle_succ_diag_l : forall n, ~ S n <= n`. The hypothesis H : S (max_list L) <= max_list L. So `exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l (max_list L) H)`. ✓

```
(* ---------- Free atoms ---------- *)

Fixpoint free_atom (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => free_atom x t1 \/ free_atom x t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => free_atom x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  end.

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar y => cons y nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => app (fv t1) (fv t2)
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Lemma fv_spec : forall t x, In x (fv t) <-> free_atom x t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i | y | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros x; simpl.
  - split; intros H; [destruct H | destruct H].
  - split; intros H.
    + destruct H as [-> | H]; [reflexivity | destruct H].
    + subst x. left. reflexivity.
  - split.
    + intros H. apply in_app_or in H. destruct H as [H | H].
      * left. apply (proj1 IH1). exact H.
      * right. apply (proj1 IH2). exact H.
    + intros [H | H].
      * apply in_or_app. left. apply (proj2 IH1). exact H.
      * apply in_or_app. right. apply (proj2 IH2). exact H.
  - split; intros H.
    + apply (proj1 IH). exact H.
    + apply (proj2 IH). exact H.
  - split; intros H; destruct H.
  - split; intros H; destruct H.
Qed.
```
Careful: the constructors order: tm_bvar, tm_fvar, tm_app, tm_abs, tm_true, tm_false. Induction gives terms t1, t2 for app, t1 for abs. Names: `induction t as [i | y | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]`. Hmm, the tm_abs constructor has arguments (ty, tm); the pattern should bind the ty too: `| T t1 IH |` ✓.

In the fvar case, `In x [y]` simplifies to `x = y \/ In x []`; `simpl` may or may not reduce `In x []` to False. `In` is defined as `In x [] = False`? `In` is defined as:
```
Fixpoint In (a:A) (l:list A) : Prop :=
  match l with
  | [] => False
  | b :: m => b = a \/ In a m
  end.
```
So `In x (y :: nil)` reduces to `y = x \/ In x nil` = `y = x \/ False`. `simpl` will reduce `In x nil` to False, and `A \/ False` might simplify to A? `simpl` can simplify `or`? Hmm, `or` is an inductive with constructors or_introl, or_intror; `A \/ False` is not definitionally A. `simpl` may reduce `or`? I don't think `simpl` reduces `A \/ False` unless there's a simplification lemma (there is `or_false_r` maybe used by `simpl`? No). So the fvar case goal is `In x (y :: nil) <-> x = y` where In unfolds to `y = x \/ In x nil` = `y = x \/ False`. My proof `destruct H as [-> | H]; [reflexivity | destruct H]` handles H : y = x \/ False. Note H : y = x, `->` gives x = y? destruct H as [-> | H] on y = x substitutes y := x, so the goal becomes x = x ✓ reflexivity. Good.
Actually, the direction: `In x [y] <-> free_atom x (fvar y) = (x = y)`. `In x (y::nil)` = `y = x \/ False`. Hmm, `y = x` vs `x = y` — need symmetry in the second direction: given x = y (from subst x), goal `y = x \/ False`; `left; reflexivity`? ```
(* ---------- Term substitution ---------- *)

Definition extend (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.

Fixpoint tsubst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tsubst sigma t1) (tsubst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (tsubst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Lemma extend_eq : forall sigma x v, extend sigma x v x = v.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma extend_neq : forall sigma x v y, x <> y -> extend sigma x v y = sigma y.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E. - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. contradiction. - reflexivity. Qed.
```
Hmm, `Nat.eqb_eq : (n =? m) = true <-> n = m`. So `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E` with E : (x =? y) = true gives E : x = y. Then contradiction. Or use `rewrite Nat.eqb_neq by assumption`. Let's write:
```
Lemma extend_neq : forall sigma x v y, x <> y -> extend sigma x v y = sigma y.
Proof.
  intros sigma x v y Hxy. unfold extend.
  rewrite Nat.eqb_neq by exact Hxy. reflexivity.
Qed.
```

```

```

Lemma lc_at_open_rec_id : forall t n, lc_at n t -> forall k v, n <= k -> open_rec k v t = t.
Proof.

  - inversion Hlc; subst. rewrite (IH1 n H1 k v Hnk), (IH2 n H2 k v Hnk). reflexivity.
  - inversion Hlc; subst. rewrite (IH (S n) H1 (S k) v).

    + lia.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_abs_inv : forall k T t, lc_at k (tm_abs T t) -> lc_at (S k) t.

Lemma value_no_step : forall v v', value v -> step v v' -> False.
Proof.
  intros v v' Hv.
  destruct Hv as [T t Hlc | | ]; intros Hstep.
  - exact (abs_not_step T t v' Hstep).
  - inversion Hstep.
  - inversion Hstep.
Qed.
```

    set (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).

Let me define:
```
Lemma fresh_both : forall L t, ~ In (fresh (L ++ fv t)) L /\ ~ free_atom (fresh (L ++ fv t)) t.
Proof.
  intros L t. assert (Hnotin : ~ In (fresh (L ++ fv t)) (L ++ fv t)) by apply fresh_notin.
  split.
  - intro Hc. apply Hnotin. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hc.
  - intro Hc. apply Hnotin. apply in_or_app. right. apply fv_spec. exact Hc.
Qed.
```

```
Lemma TermRel_lc : forall Ty t, TermRel Ty t -> locally_closed t.

H : SN t ∧ lc t ∧ (((forall u, TermRel T1 u -> SN (app t u)) ∧ (forall v, ...))). `proj2 (proj2 H)` : (forall u, ...) ∧ (forall v, ...). `proj1` : forall u, ... ✓.

Hmm, careful about the associativity: `A /\ B /\ C` parses as `A /\ (B /\ C)`. Our TermRel body is `SN t /\ lc t /\ match ... end`. The match result for Arrow is `(forall u, ...) /\ (forall v, ...)`. So the whole is `SN t /\ (lc t /\ (P /\ Q))`. ✓

```
Lemma TermRel_value_red : forall Ty t v, TermRel Ty t -> multi step t v -> value v -> ValRel Ty v.
Proof.
  intros Ty t v H Hm Hv. destruct Ty as [| T1 T2]; simpl in *.
  - exact I.
  - exact (proj2 (proj2 (proj2 H)) v Hm Hv).
Qed.
```

```
Lemma TermRel_step : forall Ty t t', TermRel Ty t -> step t t' -> TermRel Ty t'.
Proof.
  intros Ty t t' H Hstep. destruct Ty as [| T1 T2]; simpl in *.
  - destruct H as [Hsn [Hlc _]]. split.
    + exact (SN_step t t' Hsn Hstep).
    + exact (lc_step t t' Hstep Hlc).
  - destruct H as [Hsn [Hlc [Happ Hval]]]. split; [| split].
    + exact (SN_step t t' Hsn Hstep).
    + exact (lc_step t t' Hstep Hlc).
    + intros u Hu. 
      apply SN_step with (tm_app t u).
      * apply Happ. exact Hu.
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hstep | apply TermRel_lc with (Ty := T1); exact Hu].
    + intros v Hm Hv. 
      apply Hval with v.
      * apply multi_step with t'; [exact Hstep | exact Hm].
      * exact Hv.
Qed.
```
Wait: in the Arrow case, after `split; [| split]`, the goals are SN t', lc t', and `(forall u, ...) /\ (forall v, ...)` — need another split. Hmm: the goal after simpl for Arrow is `SN t' /\ lc t' /\ (P /\ Q)`. So `split; [| split]` gives SN t'; lc t'; and P /\ Q. Then we need to split P /\ Q. Let me use `split; [| split; [| split]]`:
- split → SN t' and (lc t' ∧ (P∧Q))
- second: split → lc t' and P∧Q
- third: split → P and Q.
So `split; [| split; [| split]]` yields 4 goals ✓. Hmm, does the `; [| ...]` nesting work as I think? `split; [tac1 | tac2]` applies tac1 to the first generated subgoal and tac2 to the second. So `split; [| split; [| split]]`:
- First split on goal: subgoals G1 (SN t') and G2 (lc t' ∧ (P ∧ Q)).
- tac1 is empty (first branch `[|`... hmm the syntax `split; [| split; [| split]]` means first branch empty? Actually `[| X]` with an empty first branch means "do nothing" for the first goal. Then the second branch is `split; [| split]` applied to G2:
  - split G2 → G2a (lc t') and G2b (P ∧ Q).
  - first branch empty (lc t'), second: `split` G2b → P and Q.
So total goals: SN t', lc t', P, Q ✓.

Alternatively, since Coq's `split` on `A /\ B /\ C` is right-assoc, I could use `repeat split`. Let me use explicit `split; [| split; [| split]]`.

Actually, to be safe, let me first `destruct H as [Hsn [Hlc [Happ Hval]]]`, then `split; [exact ... | split; [exact ... | split; [ ... | ...]]]`. Bullets are easier to follow but the nesting is verbose. Let me use `repeat split` — it will split all conjunctions in the goal. Since SN and lc are not conjunctions (they're inductives/definitions), `repeat split` stops. The goal `SN t' ∧ lc t' ∧ (P ∧ Q)` → repeat split gives 4 goals ✓. I'll use `repeat split`.

But careful: `repeat split` on `forall u, ...` does not split (it's not a conjunction) ✓.

Now, `lc_step`'s argument order: define as `forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'`. Then `lc_step t t' Hstep Hlc` ✓.

```
Lemma TermRel_multistep : forall Ty t t', multi step t t' -> TermRel Ty t -> TermRel Ty t'.
Proof.
  intros Ty t t' Hm. induction Hm as [x | x y z Hstep Hm IH]; intros H.
  - exact H.
  - apply IH. apply TermRel_step with y; assumption.
Qed.
```

Now T_app_abs. Let me write it carefully.

```
Lemma TermRel_app_abs : forall A B body,
  locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
  (forall v, TermRel A v -> TermRel B (open body v)) ->
  forall u, TermRel A u -> TermRel B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u).
Proof.
  intros A B body Hlc Hbody u Hu.
  destruct B as [| B1 B2].
  - (* B = Bool *)
    simpl. split.
    + apply SN_app_abs with A body.
      * intros v Hv. apply TermRel_SN with Ty_Bool. apply Hbody. exact Hv.
      * exact Hu.
    + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply TermRel_lc with A; exact Hu].
  - (* B = B1 -> B2 *)
    simpl.
    assert (Hsnapp : strongly_normalizing (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u)).
    { apply SN_app_abs with A body.
      - intros v Hv. apply TermRel_SN with (Ty_Arrow B1 B2). apply Hbody. exact Hv.
      - exact Hu. }
    assert (Hlcapp : locally_closed (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u)).
    { apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply TermRel_lc with A; exact Hu]. }
    split; [exact Hsnapp | split; [exact Hlcapp | split]].
    + (* forall w, TermRel B1 w -> SN (app (app abs u) w) *)
      intros w Hw.
      apply SN_app_of_value_reds with (R := TermRel B1) (w := w).
      * intros x y Hx Hs. apply TermRel_step with x; assumption.
      * exact Hsnapp.
      * exact Hw.
      * intros v Hm Hval x Hx.
        destruct (app_abs_multistep_value A body u v Hm Hval) as [v2 [Hm2 [Hval2 Hbody2]]].
        pose proof (Hbody v2 (TermRel_multistep A u v2 Hm2 Hu)) as HT.
        pose proof (TermRel_app_SN B1 B2 (open body v2) x HT Hx) as Hsnx.
        pose proof (multi_app_l (open body v2) v x Hbody2 (TermRel_lc B1 x Hx)) as Hmx.
        exact (SN_multistep (tm_app (open body v2) x) (tm_app v x) Hmx Hsnx).
    + (* value reducts *)
      intros v Hm Hval w Hw.
      destruct (app_abs_multistep_value A body u v Hm Hval) as [v2 [Hm2 [Hval2 Hbody2]]].
      pose proof (Hbody v2 (TermRel_multistep A u v2 Hm2 Hu)) as HT.
      exact (TermRel_value_red (Ty_Arrow B1 B2) (open body v2) v HT Hbody2 Hval w Hw).
Qed.
```
Wait, after `split; [exact Hsnapp | split; [exact Hlcapp | split]]`, the goals: Hsnapp, Hlcapp, and P ∧ Q split into P and Q. Let me check: the goal after simpl is `SN x ∧ lc x ∧ (P ∧ Q)`. `split; [exact Hsnapp | split; [exact Hlcapp | split]]`:
- split → G1 = SN x, G2 = lc x ∧ (P∧Q).
  - tactical: first branch `exact Hsnapp` closes G1.
  - second branch: `split` on G2 → lc x and P∧Q; then `[exact Hlcapp | split]` → exact Hlcapp closes lc x; split on P∧Q → P and Q.
✓ So 2 remaining goals: P and Q. Good, and my bullets + ... + ... cover them.

Hmm, but there's a subtlety in `destruct (app_abs_multistep_value A body u v Hm Hval)`: the lemma is `forall T body u v, multi step (tm_app (tm_abs T body) u) v -> value v -> exists v2, ...`. With T=A, body=body, u=u, v=v ✓.

`TermRel_multistep A u v2 Hm2 Hu` — TermRel_multistep : forall Ty t t', multi step t t' -> TermRel Ty t -> TermRel Ty t'. So `TermRel_multistep A u v2 Hm2 Hu` ✓.

`TermRel_app_SN B1 B2 (open body v2) x HT Hx` — the lemma is forall T1 T2 t u, TermRel (Arrow T1 T2) t -> TermRel T1 u -> SN (app t u). Here T1=B1, T2=B2, t = open body v2, u = x ✓.

`TermRel_value_red (Ty_Arrow B1 B2) (open body v2) v HT Hbody2 Hval` : ValRel (Arrow B1 B2) v. Then applying to w Hw: the term `TermRel_value_red ... w Hw` should have type `TermRel B2 (app v w)`. Since ValRel is a Definition and the argument (Ty_Arrow B1 B2) is a constructor, delta+iota reduction should make it a forall. Coq's application checks conversion. I think it works. Actually, since TermRel_value_red's conclusion is stated as `ValRel Ty v`, and Coq's kernel will reduce ValRel (Ty_Arrow B1 B2) v to the forall type when checking the application. Delta-reduction is part of conversion, so it should work.

Now, the fundamental theorem. Let me write it.

```
Definition env_sat (sigma : atom -> tm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> TermRel T (sigma x)).

Definition sem_ty (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (Ty : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma, env_sat sigma Gamma -> TermRel Ty (tsubst sigma t).
```

```
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t Ty, has_type Gamma t Ty -> sem_ty Gamma t Ty.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t Ty H. induction H as [Gamma x T Hx | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody_ty IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma].
```

So the pattern gives L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, Hbody_ty, IH. ✓ (The order of hypotheses in the induction: the constructor's arguments then the premise(s), with IHs generated.)

Hmm, but the actual order in the induction principle: for each constructor, the arguments are bound, then for each recursive premise an IH. Since the only premise is the function one, the pattern `[Gamma x T Hx | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody_ty IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma]` should work.

```
(** * Logical relation for strong normalization. *)

(** ** Fresh atoms *)

Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | cons x xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma max_list_ge : forall (L : list nat) (x : nat), In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl in Hin. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list L); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (max_list_ge L (S (max_list L)) Hin) as Hle.
  exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l (max_list L) Hle).
Qed.
```

Then free_atom/fv etc.

Let me write the code.

```
(** ** The logical relation *)

Fixpoint TermRel (Ty : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  locally_closed t /\
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      (forall u : tm, TermRel T1 u -> strongly_normalizing (tm_app t u)) /\
      (forall v : tm, multi step t v -> value v ->
         forall u : tm, TermRel T1 u -> TermRel T2 (tm_app v u))
  end.

Definition ValRel (Ty : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match Ty with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u : tm, TermRel T1 u -> TermRel T2 (tm_app v u)
  end.
```

Let me first test that this Fixpoint is accepted by the guard checker. Let me add just these definitions and compile.

Actually, let me add everything at once and iterate. But if the Fixpoint fails, nothing after matters. Let me add the definitions first and compile.

I'm done. Let me summarize the proof architecture concisely:

1. Infrastructure: fresh atoms (max_list/fresh), free variables (fv/free_atom), term substitution `tsubst` for term environments, local-closure lemmas (monotonicity, opening, substitution), value/step/SN basics, multistep decomposition lemmas.
2. Logical relation: `TermRel Ty t` = SN t ∧ lc t ∧ (for arrows) application to related arguments is SN, and every value reduct applied to a related argument is in the relation; `ValRel` is the value part.
3. Key lemmas: TermRel closed under step/multistep, value reducts, SN of abstractions applied to related arguments (`SN_app_abs`), a general lemma for SN of applications when all value reducts of the function are semantically good (`SN_app_of_value_reds`), and `TermRel_app_abs` which proves the beta/application obligations.
4. Substitution interpretation `env_sat` (locally closed + related at the context type) and `sem_ty`; fundamental theorem by induction on the typing derivation (cofinite T_Abs handled by choosing a fresh atom and the substitution/opening composition lemma).
5. Final theorem: instantiate the fundamental theorem with the identity substitution and the empty context.
